Knowledge Base Overview
This document is the structured visa knowledge base for the AI-Native Travel Agency Copilot. It contains 200 entries — 50 destination countries × 4 visa types per country (tourist, business, student, transit). Each entry follows the extended schema with Wakanow-style content fields plus four agentic-monitor fields: source_url, last_verified_date, change_risk_level, and version_id.
Entries are color-coded by verification status. VERIFIED entries have been researched from the official .gov source on the date shown and are safe for production use. PENDING VERIFICATION entries are scaffolded stubs with the official source URL identified; their content fields contain placeholder text and must not be used in client-facing answers until an engineer flips the verification_status field to 'verified' after a full research pass.
For GraphRAG ingestion: each entry's source_url should be embedded as the citation surface. The change_risk_level field (low / medium / high) tells the agentic change-monitor how aggressively to watch this entry's source for updates — high-risk entries (UK, US, Canada, UAE, Saudi, Qatar, Israel, China, Russia, South Africa) should be scraped weekly; medium-risk entries fortnightly; low-risk monthly.
Schema Definition
Field	Type	Purpose
country	string	Destination country name
visa_type	string	Human-readable visa name (e.g., 'Standard Visitor Visa')
visa_category	enum	tourist | business | student | transit
fees_naira_estimate	number	Estimated fees in NGN for client communication; refresh quarterly
overview	string	2-3 paragraph summary, used as the agent's narrative response base
requirements	string[]	Bullet list of eligibility conditions
required_documents	object[]	{item, requirement_level (Required|Optional|Conditional), notes}
how_to_apply	string[]	Ordered step list; rendered with Step 1/2/3 prefixes
faq	object[]	{q, a} pairs; embeddable in agent retrieval
source_url	URL	Official .gov source; serves as the citation surface in agent responses
last_verified_date	date	ISO date of last manual verification; agentic monitor compares to today
change_risk_level	enum	low | medium | high; sets scrape frequency for the agentic change-monitor
version_id	string	Semver-style identifier; bumped on every content change
verification_status	enum	verified | partial | pending; gates production use
Shared Schengen Rules
Schengen visa rules are common across all 27 Schengen states (regulation EU 2018/1806). The 17 Schengen countries covered in this KB inherit these common rules, with country-specific variations in consulate/VFS routing and the main-destination rule for application filing. The base URL is europa.eu.
Common Schengen rules: passport must be valid 3+ months after departure from Schengen area and issued within last 10 years; max stay 90 days in any 180-day window; medical insurance with EUR 30,000 minimum coverage required; uniform visa fee EUR 90 (adult) / EUR 45 (child 6-12) / free under 6; standard processing time 15 calendar days, up to 45 in complex cases; biometrics captured at VFS, valid 59 months.
Source: https://home-affairs.ec.europa.eu/policies/schengen/visa-policy/applying-schengen-visa_en
Visa Entries by Country
Entries below are grouped by destination country. Within each country, the four visa categories appear in order: Tourist/Visitor, Business, Student, Transit. The 12 verified entries (United Kingdom, United States, Canada × 4 visa types) demonstrate the full schema in production-ready depth; the remaining 188 entries are scaffolded stubs awaiting full verification from their official source URLs.
United Kingdom  ·  Europe (Anglophone)
Business Visitor Visa  (business)
VERIFIED	Source: UK Government (GOV.UK)	Version: uk-business-visitor-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	Standard Visitor (Business activities)
Processing time	3 weeks standard
Validity	6 months per visit
Fees from	GBP 135
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 270,000
Overview
Business visitors use the same Standard Visitor visa framework; the differentiation is in declared purpose and supporting documents. Permitted business activities include attending meetings and conferences, negotiating deals, attending trade fairs, gathering information for overseas work, attending interviews, and giving one-off paid engagements as an expert. Performing work for a UK company (employed or self-employed) remains prohibited.
Requirements
●	Valid passport for whole UK stay
●	Genuine business purpose that fits permitted activities
●	Funding to cover the trip without working in the UK
●	Intention to leave at end of visit
●	Proof of business or employment in Nigeria
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Valid passport	Required	Valid for whole stay; blank pages
Completed UKVI online application	Required	GWF reference
Invitation letter from UK business	Required	On company letterhead, detailing purpose, duration, and host responsibility
Letter from Nigerian employer	Required	Confirming employment, salary, leave approval, and trip purpose
Bank statements (3-6 months)	Required	Personal or company statements depending on who funds the trip
Proof of business registration (CAC)	Conditional	Required if self-employed or visiting on own business
Conference / event registration	Conditional	If attending a specific event
Travel itinerary and accommodation	Required	Flight reservation, hotel booking or host address
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Confirm the business activity fits permitted engagement rules on GOV.UK
●	Step 2:  Complete the UKVI online application declaring business purpose
●	Step 3:  Pay the GBP 135 fee and book biometrics at VFS Lagos or Abuja
●	Step 4:  Attend biometrics appointment and submit documents
●	Step 5:  Await decision (typically 3 weeks)
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: Can I sign contracts on a UK Business Visitor visa?
A: You can negotiate and sign contracts as a representative of an overseas business. You cannot do productive work for a UK company or be paid from a UK source (except permitted paid engagements).
Q: What counts as a 'permitted paid engagement'?
A: An expert invited to give a lecture, perform, examine students, or take part in a one-off event. Must be arranged before you travel and declared on the application.
Q: Can my UK host pay my expenses?
A: Yes; the host can cover reasonable travel and accommodation costs. Direct salary payment from a UK source is not allowed on a visitor visa.
Important Notes
●	Permitted business activities are strictly defined; check GOV.UK before applying
●	Misrepresenting a work visit as a business visit is a refusal trigger and damages future applications
●	Long-term 2/5/10 year business visitor visas are available for established frequent visitors

Field	Value
Source URL	https://www.gov.uk/standard-visitor/visit-on-business

Source authority	UK Government (GOV.UK)
Last verified	2026-10-24
Change risk level	medium
Version ID	uk-business-visitor-v1-2026-10
Verification status	verified

Student Visitor Visa (Short-term study)  (student)
VERIFIED	Source: UK Government (GOV.UK)	Version: uk-student-short-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	Short-term Study Visa
Processing time	3 weeks standard
Validity	6 months standard; 11 months for English language courses
Fees from	GBP 200
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 468,000
Overview
The Short-term Study visa covers courses up to 6 months (or 11 months for English language courses). For longer degree programs, the main Student Visa (formerly Tier 4) is required. Short-term study visa holders cannot work in the UK or extend their stay from inside the UK.
Requirements
●	Valid passport
●	Offer letter from a UK educational institution on the register of licensed sponsors
●	Proof of funds covering tuition and living costs
●	Genuine intention to study and leave at end of course
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Passport	Required	Valid for whole stay
CAS (Confirmation of Acceptance for Studies) or unconditional offer letter	Required	From a UK-registered institution
Bank statements	Required	Showing tuition + GBP 1,334/month (London) or GBP 1,023/month (outside London) for living costs
Academic qualifications	Required	Certificates and transcripts
TB test certificate	Required	Required for courses over 6 months
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Receive an unconditional offer + CAS from a UK institution
●	Step 2:  Complete UKVI online application and pay GBP 200 fee
●	Step 3:  Attend biometrics at VFS Lagos or Abuja
●	Step 4:  Submit documents and await decision
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: Can I work on a Short-term Study visa?
A: No. Short-term study visa holders cannot work, including part-time or internships. For work rights, apply for a full Student Visa.
Q: Can I extend a Short-term Study visa from inside the UK?
A: No. You must leave the UK and reapply from Nigeria.
Important Notes
●	Short-term study is for courses up to 6 months (or 11 months for English language courses)
●	Cannot switch to a Student Visa from inside the UK on a Short-term Study visa
●	TB test required for stays over 6 months

Field	Value
Source URL	https://www.gov.uk/study-uk

Source authority	UK Government (GOV.UK)
Last verified	2026-10-24
Change risk level	medium
Version ID	uk-student-short-v1-2026-10
Verification status	verified

Direct Airside Transit Visa (DATV)  (transit)
VERIFIED	Source: UK Government (GOV.UK)	Version: uk-transit-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	Direct Airside Transit Visa (DATV) or Visitor in Transit
Processing time	3 weeks standard
Validity	DATV airside transit; Visitor in Transit up to 48 hours
Fees from	GBP 32 (DATV); GBP 64 (Visitor in Transit)
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 64,000
Overview
Nigerian passport holders transiting through a UK airport require a DATV (airside, no UK border crossing) or a Visitor in Transit visa (landside, can pass through UK border). DATV is cheaper and applies if you stay airside and depart within 24 hours. Some exemptions exist for holders of valid US/Canada/Schengen visas.
Requirements
●	Valid passport
●	Confirmed onward flight within 24 hours (DATV) or 48 hours (Visitor in Transit)
●	Valid visa for the final destination (where applicable)
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Passport	Required	Valid for whole transit period
Onward flight booking	Required	Within 24 hours for DATV
Visa for final destination	Conditional	If final destination requires one
Proof of funds for transit period	Required	For Visitor in Transit only
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Determine whether DATV or Visitor in Transit applies
●	Step 2:  Apply online via GOV.UK
●	Step 3:  Attend biometrics at VFS Lagos or Abuja
●	Step 4:  Await decision
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: Do I need a DATV if I have a valid US visa?
A: Holders of valid US, Canada, Australia, New Zealand, or Schengen visas may qualify for transit without visa (TWOV) under specific conditions. Verify current rules on GOV.UK before travel.
Important Notes
●	DATV is airside only; you cannot clear UK border control
●	Transit without visa concessions are time-limited and route-specific; verify on GOV.UK
●	Misusing a transit visa to enter the UK leads to removal and future refusal

Field	Value
Source URL	https://www.gov.uk/transit-visa

Source authority	UK Government (GOV.UK)
Last verified	2026-10-24
Change risk level	medium
Version ID	uk-transit-v1-2026-10
Verification status	verified

Standard Visitor Visa  (tourist)
VERIFIED	Source: UK Government (GOV.UK)	Version: uk-standard-visitor-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	Standard Visitor Visa
Processing time	3 weeks standard
Validity	6 months per visit; long-term options up to 10 years
Fees from	GBP 135
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 270,000
Overview
Most Nigerian travelers to the UK need a Standard Visitor visa for tourism, family visits, or short business trips. Applications are submitted online via GOV.UK with biometrics captured at a VFS centre in Lagos or Abuja. Permitted activities include tourism, volunteering up to 30 days, attending meetings/interviews, certain paid engagements as an expert, recreational courses up to 30 days, and study up to 6 months. Working for a UK company (paid or unpaid), claiming public funds, marrying, or living in the UK through frequent visits are prohibited.
Requirements
●	Valid passport for the whole stay in the UK
●	Plan to leave the UK at the end of your visit
●	Able to support yourself without recourse to public funds
●	Able to pay for the return onward journey
●	Genuine visitor intent (no work, no settlement)
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Valid passport	Required	Valid for whole UK stay; blank pages for visa sticker
Completed UKVI online application	Required	GWF reference generated on submission
Recent passport photo (UK specification)	Required	Digital photo uploaded during application
Bank statements (3-6 months)	Required	Personal or sponsor; shows ability to fund the trip
Employment / business proof	Required	Letter from employer on letterhead, or CAC documents for self-employed, or tax records
Accommodation and travel plans	Required	Hotel booking, host address, or letter of invitation from UK host
TB test certificate	Conditional	Required if staying over 6 months or for certain routes; Nigeria is on the TB screening list
Travel history (last 10 years)	Conditional	May be requested depending on circumstances
Criminal / civil / immigration offence declarations	Required	Declared on the application form
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Check if a visa is required at gov.uk and confirm Standard Visitor is the correct category
●	Step 2:  Complete the UKVI online application on GOV.UK and pay the visa fee
●	Step 3:  Book a biometrics appointment at a VFS centre in Lagos or Abuja
●	Step 4:  Attend the appointment; fingerprints and photo captured; passport returned same day
●	Step 5:  Upload supporting documents per VFS instructions
●	Step 6:  Receive decision by email typically within 3 weeks; passport returned via courier
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: How much bank balance is needed for a UK Standard Visitor visa?
A: There is no fixed amount. The caseworker must be satisfied you can cover the trip cost (flights, accommodation, daily expenses) without recourse to public funds. A common rule of thumb is a closing balance that comfortably exceeds the projected trip cost.
Q: Can I apply for a 2-year, 5-year, or 10-year long-term visitor visa?
A: Yes, but the fee is materially higher (GBP 506/GBP 903/GBP 1,128). Long-term visas are granted only if you can demonstrate a sustained pattern of frequent UK visits. If UKVI believes you cannot meet the eligibility for the full duration, they may grant a shorter visa with no refund.
Q: Can I work on a Standard Visitor visa?
A: No. You cannot do paid or unpaid work for a UK company or as a self-employed person, except for permitted paid engagements as an invited expert in your profession.
Q: Is a TB test required for Nigerians?
A: Yes for stays over 6 months and for certain routes. Nigeria is on the UK TB screening list; tests must be done at an approved clinic.
Q: Can I extend my Standard Visitor visa from inside the UK?
A: Only in exceptional circumstances (e.g., medical treatment). Extensions are not granted routinely; you must leave and reapply from Nigeria for the next visit.
Important Notes
●	Honest travel history materially improves credibility; do not omit previous refusals
●	TB test required for stays over 6 months; book at an approved clinic before biometrics
●	Visa fees are non-refundable even if the application is refused
●	Long-term visas (2/5/10 year) may be granted at a shorter duration than requested if UKVI is not satisfied with your travel pattern
●	Repeated long visits or near-consecutive stays risk visa cancellation

B-1 Business Visitor Visa  (business)

Validity	Up to 10 years multiple entry
Fees from	USD 185 MRV fee
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 295,000
Overview
The B-1 visa is for business visitors attending meetings, conferences, negotiating contracts, consulting clients, or attending trade shows. The B-1/B-2 is typically issued as a combined visa. B-1 holders cannot engage in productive work or receive US-source salary.
Requirements
●	Valid passport
●	DS-160 completed
●	Genuine business purpose
●	Strong ties to Nigeria
●	Funds to cover trip without US-source employment
Required Documents

●	Step 4:  Receive passport with visa via courier
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: Can I sign contracts on B-1?
A: Yes, as a representative of an overseas business. You cannot do productive work for a US company or receive US-source salary.
Q: Can I attend a trade show on B-1?
A: Yes, attending trade shows and negotiating contracts are permitted B-1 activities.
Important Notes

F-1 Academic Student Visa  (student)
VERIFIED	Source: US Department of State	Version: us-f1-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	F-1 Student Visa
Processing time	Interview scheduling varies; passport return 5-15 working days post-interview
Validity	Program duration (D/S); visa typically 5 years
Fees from	USD 185 MRV + USD 350 SEVIS
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 560,000
Overview
F-1 is the standard academic student visa for full-time study at a US Student and Exchange Visitor Program (SEVP)-certified institution. Requires a Form I-20 from the school, SEVIS I-901 fee payment, and a consular interview.
Requirements
●	Form I-20 from SEVP-certified school
●	SEVIS I-901 fee paid
●	DS-160 completed
●	Proof of funds for tuition + living costs
●	Strong ties to Nigeria
●	Genuine intent to study and depart
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Form I-20	Required	Issued by the SEVP-certified school after admission and proof of funds
SEVIS I-901 fee receipt	Required	USD 350 paid at fmjfee.com
DS-160 confirmation	Required	With school details
Passport	Required	6+ months validity
Academic transcripts and certificates	Required	From previous institutions
Standardized test scores	Conditional	TOEFL/IELTS/SAT/GRE as required by school
Bank statements / sponsor affidavit	Required	Showing 1 year of tuition + living costs
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Receive admission + Form I-20 from a SEVP-certified US school
●	Step 2:  Pay SEVIS I-901 fee (USD 350) at fmjfee.com
●	Step 3:  Complete DS-160 and pay MRV fee (USD 185)
●	Step 4:  Schedule and attend interview at US Embassy/Consulate
●	Step 5:  Receive F-1 visa; enter US up to 30 days before program start
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: Can I work on F-1?
A: Yes, limited on-campus employment (20 hours/week during semester). Off-campus work requires authorization (CPT/OPT) and is restricted.
Q: Can I bring dependents?
A: Yes on F-2 visas for spouse and unmarried children under 21. F-2 dependents cannot work.
Important Notes
●	Maintain full-time enrollment to maintain F-1 status
●	D/S notation means visa validity is tied to program duration
●	Visa can be refused if SEVIS fee is unpaid or I-20 is incomplete

Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 295,000
Overview
C-1 is the US transit visa for travelers passing through the US en route to a third country. Requires a confirmed onward ticket within a reasonable transit window. Cannot be used to enter the US for any other purpose.
Requirements

●	Confirmed onward ticket to a third country
●	Valid visa for final destination

●	Step 4:  Receive C-1 visa
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: Can I leave the airport on a C-1 visa?
A: C-1 allows entry through US border, but the stay is limited to a reasonable transit window (typically under 24 hours). You cannot use C-1 for tourism or business.
Important Notes
●	C-1 is transit only; misusing it for tourism leads to inadmissibility
●	Visa validity does not extend the permitted transit period

B-1/B-2 Visitor Visa  (tourist)
VERIFIED	Source: US Department of State (travel.state.gov)	Version: us-b1b2-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	B-1/B-2 Visitor Visa
Processing time	5-15 working days after successful interview
Validity	Up to 10 years multiple entry
Fees from	USD 185 MRV fee
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 295,000
Overview
Nigerian citizens need a valid US non-immigrant visa for tourism, business, or family travel. The B-1/B-2 is the most common visitor visa category. Applications are submitted via the DS-160 online form on ceac.state.gov, with biometrics and interview at the US Embassy in Abuja or US Consulate General in Lagos.
Requirements
●	Valid Nigerian passport (6+ months validity beyond intended stay)
●	Completed DS-160 confirmation page
●	Recent passport photograph (US visa specification)
●	Proof of strong ties to Nigeria (employment, business, family, property)
●	Financial evidence for trip costs
●	Travel itinerary and accommodation details
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
International passport	Required	Bio-data page + previous visas if any
DS-160 confirmation page	Required	Barcode page from ceac.state.gov
Appointment confirmation	Required	CGI appointment letter for embassy/consulate
Bank statements (3-6 months, stamped)	Required	Personal; reflects capacity to fund trip
Employment letter	Required	On company letterhead with salary and leave approval
Invitation letter	Optional	If visiting family or for business meetings in the US
Pay slips (last 3 months)	Conditional	Strengthens ties-to-home-country narrative
Property / asset documents	Optional	Strengthens ties-to-home-country narrative
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Check eligibility and confirm B-1/B-2 fits travel purpose
●	Step 2:  Complete DS-160 online at ceac.state.gov; print confirmation barcode
●	Step 3:  Pay the USD 185 MRV fee at a designated bank (e.g., GTBank)
●	Step 4:  Schedule interview at US Embassy Abuja or US Consulate Lagos via CGI
●	Step 5:  Attend interview with originals and copies; biometrics captured same day
●	Step 6:  If approved, passport returned via courier typically 5-15 working days
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: How long does a US visa take from Nigeria?
A: Interview slot availability varies by season; once interviewed and approved, passport return typically takes 5-15 working days. Wait times for interview slots can extend to several months in peak seasons.
Q: Can a travel agent guarantee US visa approval?
A: No. No travel agent can guarantee approval. The consular officer makes the decision based on the applicant's profile, ties to Nigeria, and stated purpose. Agents ensure completeness and accuracy of the application.
Q: Where is the US visa interview held for Nigerian applicants?
A: Interviews are held at the US Embassy in Abuja or the US Consulate General in Lagos, depending on appointment availability and the applicant's profile.
Q: Do I need a return ticket before applying?
A: A confirmed or tentative return itinerary helps demonstrate intent to return. Do not purchase non-refundable tickets before visa approval.
Important Notes
●	Honest declarations of previous refusals are critical; omissions are routinely discovered and lead to lifetime bans
●	Nigerian applicants are subject to stricter review of ties-to-home-country
●	Visa validity up to 10 years does not permit stays longer than permitted by CBP at the port of entry (typically up to 6 months)
●	MRV fee is non-refundable regardless of decision
●	Reciprocity fees may apply for certain visa categories; check travel.state.gov/country-reciprocity

Study Permit  (student)
VERIFIED	Source: Government of Canada (IRCC)	Version: ca-study-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	Study Permit
Processing time	6-12 weeks typical
Validity	Program duration + 90 days
Fees from	CAD 150 + CAD 85 biometrics
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 230,000
Overview
A Study Permit is required for any program of study over 6 months in Canada. Requires a Letter of Acceptance from a Designated Learning Institution (DLI), proof of funds, and biometrics.
Requirements
●	Letter of Acceptance from a DLI
●	Proof of funds (tuition + CAD 20,635/year living costs for 2024+, plus travel)
●	Guaranteed Investment Certificate (GIC) for SDS stream
●	Medical exam
●	Police certificate
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Letter of Acceptance	Required	From a DLI
Proof of funds	Required	Tuition + CAD 20,635/year for living, plus travel
GIC (CAD 20,635)	Conditional	Required for Student Direct Stream (SDS)
IELTS / TEF scores	Conditional	SDS requires IELTS 6.0+
Medical exam	Required	Designated panel physician
Police certificate	Required	From Nigeria and any country of residence in last 10 years
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Receive Letter of Acceptance from a DLI
●	Step 2:  Pay first year tuition (or get proof of payment)
●	Step 3:  Purchase GIC (for SDS stream)
●	Step 4:  Complete medical exam and obtain police certificate
●	Step 5:  Apply online via IRCC portal
●	Step 6:  Pay fees and submit biometrics
●	Step 7:  Await decision
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: What is the Student Direct Stream (SDS)?
A: An expedited processing stream for residents of specific countries (including Nigeria) who meet stricter financial, language, and admission requirements. Decisions typically within 20 calendar days.
Q: Can I work on a Study Permit?
A: Yes, up to 24 hours/week off-campus during academic sessions, full-time during scheduled breaks. Co-op work permit required for program-mandated work.
Important Notes
●	Living cost requirement increased to CAD 20,635/year for applications from January 2024
●	SDS stream requires upfront IELTS 6.0+ and GIC; standard stream is slower but more flexible
●	Study Permit does not by itself authorize re-entry; TRV is also required for re-entry

Temporary Resident Visa (TRV)  (tourist)
VERIFIED	Source: Government of Canada (IRCC)	Version: ca-trv-v1-2026-10

Key Facts
Field	Value
Visa type	Temporary Resident Visa (TRV)
Processing time	4-8 weeks
Validity	Up to 10 years or passport expiry
Fees from	CAD 100 + CAD 85 biometrics
Fees in Naira (estimate)	NGN 295,000
Overview
Nigerian citizens need a TRV to visit Canada for tourism, family visits, or short business trips. Applications are submitted online via the IRCC portal with biometrics at a Visa Application Centre (VAC) in Nigeria.
Requirements
●	Valid passport
●	Genuine visitor intent
●	Strong ties to Nigeria
●	Proof of funds
●	Medical exam (for stays over 6 months)
Required Documents
Document	Status	Notes
Passport scans	Required	All pages with stamps/visas
Digital photo (IRCC specification)	Required	Spec compliant
Purpose of travel letter	Required	Detailed cover letter
Bank statements (6 months recommended)	Required	Stamped by bank
Employment letter / business proof	Required	Salary, leave approval
Travel itinerary	Required	Flight + accommodation
Letter of invitation	Conditional	If visiting family or business
How to Apply
●	Step 1:  Create an IRCC account online
●	Step 2:  Complete the IMM 5257 form and upload documents
●	Step 3:  Pay the visa fee (CAD 100) and biometrics fee (CAD 85)
●	Step 4:  Submit biometrics at the VAC in Lagos or Abuja
●	Step 5:  Await decision (4-8 weeks typical)
●	Step 6:  Passport request issued if approved; submit passport for visa stamping
Frequently Asked Questions
Q: What is a strong ties letter?
A: Evidence you will return to Nigeria: stable employment, business ownership, property, family responsibilities. Critical for TRV success given Nigerian applicants' elevated refusal rates.
Q: Can I visit the US and Canada on one trip?
A: Yes, but you need separate visas. Both applications can be processed in parallel.
Q: Is a medical exam required?
A: Required for stays over 6 months and for certain routes (e.g., working in healthcare). Designated panel physician in Nigeria.
Important Notes
●	Declare all previous visa refusals (US, Schengen, UK) on the IRCC forms; non-disclosure is a refusal trigger
●	Medical exam may be required for stays over 6 months
●	Family of 5+ applying at the same time: maximum total fee of CAD 500 for visas (plus biometrics)
●	Biometrics valid for 10 years; reuse for future TRV applications

Schengen common rules apply: passport valid 3+ months after departure, medical insurance with EUR 30,000 minimum coverage, biometrics at VFS Lagos or Abuja, apply at the consulate of the main destination country. Verify country-specific consular procedures and VFS routing at the official source.
